### TL;DR

The paper introduces a symmetry-based framework using isotypic measurements to systematically detect entanglement partitions in multipartite quantum systems.

### Motivation

Detecting and characterizing multipartite entanglement is a fundamental challenge, and existing criteria are often ad hoc or limited to specific cases.

### Method

The authors exploit inherent symmetries of multipartite systems and derive matrix inequalities from isotypic measurements to characterize entanglement partitions.

### TL;DR

This paper demonstrates that a Floquet-driven many-body system with a Rydberg-like blockade can exhibit area-law entanglement scaling even in a quantum chaotic regime, presenting a counterexample to the typical volume-law expectation.

### Motivation

To challenge the conventional wisdom that quantum chaos universally leads to volume-law entanglement entropy in highly excited eigenstates.

### Method

The authors study a Floquet-driven system with a Rydberg-like blockade, computing entanglement entropy of eigenstates and analyzing its scaling with system size.

### Result

Despite the system being quantum chaotic (as indicated by its level statistics), the entanglement entropy follows an area law rather than the expected volume law.

### Conclusion

This finding provides a striking counterexample that questions the direct connection between quantum chaos and volume-law entanglement, offering new insights into the interplay of driving, interactions, and entanglement structure.

*Keywords: entanglement entropy, quantum chaos, area-law, Floquet system, Rydberg blockade*

### Motivation

To understand how local monitoring affects entanglement dynamics, contrasting with unitary and post-selected (no-click) limits.

### Method

Analyzes quantum jump trajectories of a free-fermion chain with single-site density monitoring, computing entanglement entropy and waiting-time statistics.

### Result

Entanglement entropy grows to a stationary volume-law state, explained by super-Poissonian waiting times with bunching and long dark intervals; increasing monitored sites drives a transition to area-law scaling via the Zeno effect.

The presence of dark intervals is crucial for extensive entanglement growth, and the effect is suppressed when monitoring is extended to more sites.

### Result

The protocol remains effective beyond the ideal fast-control limit and preserves the characteristic enhancement of charging power, which scales with the number of battery units, in the many-body case.

### TL;DR

This paper optimizes measurement schedules for dynamically condensed colour codes under noise bias, introduces the teraquop volume metric, and shows that repeating measurements helps under measurement-biased noise while belief matching decoding can significantly improve performance.

### Motivation

To enable large-scale quantum computation with dynamical stabilizer codes, it is crucial to optimize measurement schedules, especially for hardware where measurements are more error-prone than other operations.

### Method

The authors analyze dynamically condensed colour codes, generalize the teraquop footprint to a teraquop volume that accounts for both qubit number and measurement rounds, and compare decoding strategies (belief matching vs. minimum-weight perfect matching) across different noise bias limits.

### Result

Repeating measurements improves performance for measurement-biased noise models but offers little benefit for unbiased or [MATH]-biased noise; using belief matching can turn a worst-performing code into a best-performing one under certain noise models.

### Conclusion

The teraquop volume is a more informative metric than footprint alone, as performance differences are largely due to measurement rounds, and the choice of decoder critically affects code performance.

*Keywords: dynamical stabilizer codes, measurement schedule optimization, teraquop volume, belief matching, noise bias*

### TL;DR

The paper introduces a Floquetification procedure that transforms arbitrary stabiliser codes into Floquet codes using only single- and two-qubit operations, while preserving code distance and logical count via distance-preserving rewrites in ZX calculus.

### Motivation

Large-weight stabiliser measurements are difficult to implement fault-tolerantly, so the authors aim to generate Floquet codes with only low-weight operations from any stabiliser code.

### Method

They define distance-preserving rewrites in ZX calculus and use them to decompose high-weight stabiliser measurements into circuits with single- and two-qubit gates, ensuring that a single error on data qubits propagates to at most one error.

### Result

The Floquetification preserves the distance and number of logicals of the original code, with an overhead linear in the largest measurement weight, and extends previous methods to arbitrary stabiliser codes.

### Conclusion

This work provides a general framework for constructing low-weight Floquet codes, potentially improving fault-tolerant implementations of quantum error correction.

### TL;DR

This paper demonstrates that with a suitable catalyst state, the T-depth of any single-qubit z-rotation can be reduced to a constant, enabling constant-T-depth approximations of multi-qubit gates.

### Motivation

Reducing T-depth is crucial for fault-tolerant quantum computing since T gates are expensive. The paper explores catalytic approaches to achieve constant T-depth for common unitaries.

### Method

The authors construct a catalyst state and show that for any single-qubit z-rotation, there exists a Clifford+T circuit with constant T-depth that approximates it using the catalyst. They prove the required catalyst size scales polynomially with the target precision and that the catalyst can be prepared in polynomial time.

### Result

Any single-qubit z-rotation can be approximated with constant T-depth using a polynomial-sized catalyst. This also implies that multi-qubit Toffoli, adder, and quantum Fourier transform can be approximated with constant T-depth circuits.

### Conclusion

The result improves the efficiency of quantum circuits by reducing T-depth without increasing resource overhead beyond polynomial, suggesting practical benefits for fault-tolerant quantum computation.

*Keywords: catalyst state, T-depth, Clifford+T gates, magic states, quantum circuit optimization*

### TL;DR

This paper compares two leading surface code lattice surgery compilation schemes for Hamiltonian simulation, showing that the optimal approach depends on the simulation algorithm (Trotter-Suzuki vs. quantum signal processing).

### Motivation

Fault-tolerant quantum computing resource estimates often assume a particular surface code compilation method, but it is unclear if one scheme is universally better. This work investigates whether direct Clifford+T compilation or serialized compilation (eliminating Clifford gates) yields lower resource costs for Hamiltonian simulation.

### Method

The authors analyze resource costs (e.g., T-count, spacetime volume) for simulating transverse-field Ising, Kitaev honeycomb, and a time-dependent magnetic field model, using both compilation schemes and two simulation algorithms (Trotter-Suzuki and quantum signal processing).

### Result

Trotterization benefits by orders of magnitude from direct Clifford+T compilation, while quantum signal processing may favor the serialized approach. The optimal scheme depends on circuit-level properties like logical qubit count, T fraction, and average circuit density.

### Conclusion

Surface code quantum processors should adopt adaptive compilation strategies that predict the best scheme based on high-level circuit characteristics, rather than relying on a one-size-fits-all method.

*Keywords: surface code, lattice surgery, Hamiltonian simulation, resource estimation, Clifford+T compilation*

### TL;DR

This paper analyzes resource requirements for quantum simulation of nuclear dynamics using first quantization and pionless EFT Hamiltonians.

### Motivation

Classical simulation of nuclear dynamics is exponentially hard, and quantum computers may enable scalable simulations; previous work in second quantization had high resource costs.

### Method

The authors employ first quantization with the full LO pionless EFT Hamiltonian and analyze time evolution using product formulas and quantum signal processing, deriving asymptotic resource estimates.

### Result

They show polynomial scaling in particle number and logarithmic scaling in single-particle basis states, an exponential improvement over second quantization; realistic low-energy scattering simulations require ~tens of millions of T gates and a few hundred logical qubits.

### Conclusion

The study suggests that early fault-tolerant quantum computers could feasibly simulate simple nuclear reactions, highlighting the promise of quantum simulation for nuclear physics.

*Keywords: quantum simulation, first quantization, pionless EFT, quantum signal processing, product formulas*

To reconcile non-relativistic decomposition of angular momentum into intrinsic and extrinsic parts with relativistic quantum mechanics, particularly addressing the issue of massless particles.

### Method

### Motivation

Axion dark matter searches require low backgrounds; cosmic-ray muons passing through the magnetic field can emit synchrotron radiation that might mimic axion signals.

### Method

They use GEANT4 simulations to model muon tracks in an 8 T solenoid and also derive analytical formulas for the synchrotron radiation power spectrum.

They find that for eV-scale axion experiments, the background is not dominant due to high Q and fine energy resolution, but broadband experiments without sufficient resolution are vulnerable.

## 384. The Paradox Of Steady-state Light Diffusion

- **Score**: 0.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Rep. Prog. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-08-10
- **Authors**: André Liemert, Lorenzo Pattelli, Alwin Kienle, Federico Tommasi And Fabrizio Martelli
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8eaa](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8eaa)
- **Tags**: `k`  `transport`  `convergence`  `retrieval`  `attenuation`

### TL;DR

This paper analyzes the paradox of steady-state light diffusion and shows that no scalar diffusion coefficient can make diffusion asymptotically equivalent to the radiative transfer equation when absorption is nonzero, establishing time-domain formulation as the only self-consistent reference.

### Motivation

The definition of the diffusion coefficient in light transport has been debated for decades, with absorption-dependent formulations violating a fundamental scaling property of the RTE, and the controversy remains unresolved in the steady-state regime.

### Method

The authors use an exact consistency test based on the requirement that diffusion converges to the RTE in the limit of infinite propagation, and provide analytical proofs for both time-domain and continuous-wave regimes.

### Result

In time-domain, the absorption-independent coefficient is necessary and sufficient for exact long-time convergence; in continuous-wave, no scalar diffusion coefficient can make diffusion asymptotically equivalent to the RTE with nonzero absorption.

### Conclusion

The time-domain formulation is the only self-consistent reference for diffusion in optical transport, and absorption-dependent steady-state diffusion models should be re-examined.

*Keywords: light diffusion, radiative transfer equation, absorption, time-domain, continuous-wave*

---

### TL;DR

The paper demonstrates a mechanism where Polyakov confinement transforms a Dirac spin liquid into a symmetry-protected topological state, with variational Monte Carlo evidence.

### Motivation

Understanding how fractionalized excitations reconfine into new phases of matter is a fundamental challenge in condensed matter physics.

### Method

They use variational Monte Carlo simulations on a triangular antiferromagnet with Dzyaloshinskii–Moriya interaction, analyzing Wilson loops and topological entanglement entropy.

### Result

They find a confined SPT phase characterized by an area law for Wilson loops and vanishing topological entanglement entropy, and predict a measurable spin-pump response.

### Conclusion

This work reveals a previously unexplored pathway to SPT physics, bridging gauge theory, quantum magnetism, and topological matter.

*Keywords: Polyakov confinement, Dirac spin liquid, symmetry-protected topological (SPT) phase, variational Monte Carlo, topological entanglement entropy*

Identifies a mechanistic link where ST6GAL1 alters sialyl linkages on TAMs, leading to the formation of cancer-promoting α2,6-sialo-protrusions.

### Conclusion

Highlights ST6GAL1 as a potential therapeutic target for cancer immunotherapy by modulating TAM glycosylation.

Savaspeter Penzesadepartment Of Neuroscience, Northwestern University Feinberg School Of Medicine, Chicago, IL 60611bdepartment Of Neurology, Northwestern University Feinberg School Of Medicine, Chicago, IL 60611cdepartment Of Psychiatry And Behavioral Sciences, Northwestern University Feinberg School Of Medicine, Chicago, IL 60611ddepartment Of Pharmacology, Northwestern University Feinberg School Of Medicine, Chicago, IL 60611ecenter For Autism And Neurodevelopment, Northwestern University Feinberg School Of Medicine, Chicago, IL 60611
- **Link**: [https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2606900123?af=R](https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2606900123?af=R)
- **Tags**: `k`  `issue`  `disorder`  `Ank3`  `ankyrin-G`

### TL;DR

This paper studies the effects of Ank3 deletion in adult excitatory neurons on behavior, neuronal activity, membrane proteome, and myelination in mice, linking to bipolar disorder phenotypes.

### Motivation

The study aims to understand the role of ankyrin-G (Ank3) in adult brain function, given its genetic association with bipolar disorder.

### Method

Conditional knockout of Ank3 in adult forebrain excitatory neurons in mice, followed by behavioral assays, electrophysiology, proteomics, and myelin staining.

### Result

Ank3 loss caused behavioral abnormalities resembling bipolar disorder, reduced neuronal activity, alterations in the membrane proteome, and disrupted myelination.

### Conclusion

Ank3 in adult excitatory neurons is essential for maintaining normal behavioral, neuronal, and myelination processes, providing mechanistic insight into bipolar disorder.

*Keywords: Ank3, ankyrin-G, bipolar disorder, neuronal activity, myelination, membrane proteome*
